court_idint32
CAP integer id of the court (metadata.csv court_id).e.g. 15176
reporterstring
CAP short name of the reporter series the opinion is published in (reporter__short_name), e.g. 'A.2d', 'Mass. App. Dec.'; 413 reporters.e.g. Mass. App. Dec.
reporter_idint32
CAP integer id of the reporter (metadata.csv reporter_id).e.g. 579
name_abbreviationstring
CAP abbreviated case name (e.g. 'Levenson v. Bertolet'); contains the names of the parties, often private individuals.Example withheld: the values carry people’s names.
ref_countint32
Number of cases in this corpus that the case cites: its out-degree in Edge_list.parquet (source cites target), no time window; 0 for 372,314 cases. Citations to authorities outside the CAP graph are not counted.e.g. 13
case_sb.parquet
Case law/output		3,787,86130.30 MBOne document.
4 columns · meaning and example
case_idint64
Caselaw Access Project (CAP) integer case id (metadata.csv id; values 1-12,707,012, 5,179,698 cases exist). Only cases with at least one dated citation appear (3,787,861).e.g. 1
SB_Bfloat
Ke et al. (2015) beauty coefficient B from the yearly citation counts c_t at age t = citing decision year - decision year (t >= 0): sum over t = 0..t_m of the gap between the straight line from (0, c_0) to the first peak (t_m, c_m) and c_t, divided by max(c_t, 1). Unitless; 0 when the peak is in the decision year, and it can be negative.e.g. 5
SB_Tint16
Awakening time in years since decision: the age t in [0, t_m] whose (t, c_t) lies farthest from the line joining (0, c_0) and the peak (t_m, c_m); 0 when the peak is at age 0.e.g. 10
n_citeint32
Number of dated citations the curve was built from (all citing cases with lag >= 0, equal to case_citation.C_all). B is noisy for small n_cite, so filter on it.e.g. 3

Metrics

8 families · 3–4 windows each

Every metric is computed at four horizons — 3, 5, 10 years and the full history — so that a result can always be re-stated on a truncation-safe window. Papers and patents get the same families wherever the underlying graph allows it.

Citation counts

C3 C5 C10 Call

Forward citations inside a fixed window after publication or grant.

Papers, patents and patent→paper all carry the same four windows. Patents additionally split every window by examiner / non-examiner / unknown, carry pre-grant application citations (appC), and a de-duplicated variant (uniqueC) that counts each citing patent once.

Disruption

CD3 CD5 CD10 CDall

Funk & Owen-Smith CD index: does later work cite this instead of its references (disruptive, → +1) or alongside them (consolidating, → −1)?

Stored with its counting terms ni (citing the focal work only), nj (citing both) and nk (citing the references only), so any variant can be recomputed without touching the graph.

F / E / G

F E G

Foundation, Extension, Generalization — the three-way decomposition of the disruption index introduced by Fang & Evans (2025), arXiv:2510.03240. Each citer is compared on how many of the focal work's references it also cites and how many of the focal work's other citers it cites: more citers is Foundation, more references is Extension, neither is Generalization, and a tie counts half to each.

They sum to exactly 1 by construction, which §7 of the patent notebook uses as a build check.

Atypicality

Z_median Z_10pct Z_min n_pairs

Uzzi et al. journal-pair z-scores against a degree-preserving null. Median Z = conventionality, 10th-percentile Z = tail novelty.

Papers use journal pairs; patents use CPC pairs (Kim et al. 2016 style). The pairwise z-scores are kept in a separate z_score_pair table.

Sleeping Beauty

SB_B SB_T

Ke et al. beauty coefficient B and awakening time T from the full citation history.

B measures how far the real trajectory sits below the straight line to its peak; T is where that gap is widest.

Hit probability

pctl_c3 pctl_c5 pctl_c10 pctl_call

Percentile rank of citations within a cohort, so "top 1%" means top 1% of comparable work.

Cohort = FoS × publication year for papers, WIPO sector × grant year for patents.

Citation trend

p2p pat2p pat2pat appC

One row per (document, citing year): the full accrual history rather than a single total.

584M rows on the paper side alone. Every trajectory and convexity result is computed out-of-core from this table.

Convexity index

1 − mean(normalised citation age)

Shape of the min–max scaled cumulative citation curve. Below 0.5 = convex (late burst); above = concave (early peak, saturating).

Introduced by the validation notebooks rather than by a published paper — it is the quantity that makes "sleeping beauty" and "flash in the pan" comparable on one axis.

External agreement

in progress paper_validation §3c

The strongest validity evidence available: SciSciNet computes the same metrics on MAG, this pipeline computes them on OpenAlex 2026-01. Joining on the paper id and correlating says which metrics survive a change of citation graph. Only Spearman rank correlation is reported here — almost every downstream use depends on the ordering of papers, not on the absolute value, and rank agreement is the claim that actually transfers between two different citation graphs.

See the updated Agreement with SciSciNet figure (§3c) for metric-specific correlations, sample sizes and coverage from the current validation run. Earlier restricted-run atypicality results are shown separately in §9d. The Dimensions section (§14) compares the same metrics across the OpenAlex and Dimensions indices, the PATSTAT section (§16) matches US grants against PatentsView, and the cross-check compares the disruption kernel with an independent implementation.

Validation figures

143 figures · 11 notebooks

There is no gold standard for any of these metrics, so validation is face validity: each figure either reproduces a published result, or checks an invariant the build must satisfy. The 11 notebooks are kept apart below, because they are validated against different literature and different invariants; the last one cross-checks the disruption kernel against an independent implementation. Only figures exported by the validation notebooks are shown. Click any figure to enlarge it.

Papers

OpenAlex 18 figures · paper_validation.ipynb

Validated against the paper literature: the Funk & Owen-Smith disruption distribution, the Wu et al. team-size result, the Uzzi atypicality 2×2, and Ke et al. sleeping beauties — plus the convexity tests the notebooks add on top.

Papers — Dimensions

Dimensions 12 figures · dimension_validation.ipynb

The OpenAlex paper pipeline re-run, kernel for kernel, on the Dimensions June 2025 index: 153.8 M publications and 2.14 B reference edges. Fields are ANZSRC FoR 2020 divisions, a journal is source_titles.type = "journal", and the patent→paper channel is Dimensions' own patent index rather than Reliance on Science, so a difference from the OpenAlex figures is a property of the index, not of the metric. Section numbers follow case_law_validation so the families read side by side; a section whose Dimensions input has not been written yet is absent rather than empty.

Patents

PatentsView 35 figures · patent_validation.ipynb

No external gold standard exists for the patent metrics, so this is the invariant-heavy notebook: window nesting, the F + E + G = 1 identity, citation truncation by grant year, and the examiner / applicant and granted / pre-grant splits that only the patent side has.

Patents — PATSTAT

PATSTAT 18 figures · patstat_validation.ipynb

The PatentsView pipeline re-run, kernel for kernel, on PATSTAT Global 2023 Autumn: 84.9 M applications for patents of invention from every office, 401.6 M resolved citation edges. It is stored three ways: the filing clock (both ends of a citation dated by filing year, every application; the PATSTAT / OECD convention), the grant clock (grant years, granted applications only, 44.8 M; PatentsView's convention) and the DOCDB family unit (52.8 M families: an invention filed at several offices is one node, citations are distinct family-to-family pairs, both ends dated by the earliest priority year; the OECD / EPO convention for counting inventions). Sections 1–14 read the filing clock at application level unless a panel says otherwise; §15 compares the two clocks on the same granted applications, §16 matches US grants to PatentsView by patent number and §17–18 compare applications with families. Inventor-country distributions of PATSTAT and PatentsView are in the Patent countries gallery.

Patent → paper

Reliance on Science 4 figures · pcs_validation.ipynb

How often US patents cite each paper. The checks here are about accrual and coverage: patent citations arrive an order of magnitude more slowly than paper citations, so the short windows are nearly empty by construction rather than by error.

Paper–patent pairs

PPP 12 figures · ppp_validation.ipynb

The 548,315-pair plus list links scientific and technological contributions. These validation figures join the document-level paper and patent citation histories, then apply figure-specific eligibility filters; the average cumulative trajectory in §5b covers 107,260 pairs. The separately inventoried PPP trend files are pair-level outputs. The hypothesis under test is that the paper side is convex and the patent side concave.

Case law

CASE 16 figures · case_law_validation.ipynb

5,179,698 US court opinions and 47,519,638 citations between them, 1800–2020. The same estimators as the paper and patent families — citation windows, the CD index and its F/E/G decomposition, the beauty coefficient — on a network whose citation culture is different: precedent is cited immediately and then, occasionally, rediscovered a century later.

Data provenance. The citation network and metadata were provided by the authors of Lee, S., Kim, T., Yoon, J., & Youn, H. (2024). When Common Law Ages: Two Centuries of Growing Inertia in US Judicial Opinions. arXiv preprint arXiv:2410.04493. Any use of the case_* outputs should cite it.

Author countries

OpenAlex 10 figures · author_country_validation.ipynb

Where the authors of 251.7 M OpenAlex works sit: ISO2 countries from the institution each affiliation resolved to, one row per work in paper_author_country.parquet. 44.6% of works have at least one located author. Coverage, country shares under full and fractional counting, international collaboration by team size, its relation to impact and disruption, partner pairs, country profiles, and a cross-check against the Dimensions author countries.

Inventor countries

PatentsView 9 figures · inventor_country_validation.ipynb

Where the inventors of 8.5 M US utility patents sit: the country of the address printed on the grant, one row per patent in patent_inventor_country.parquet, with the assignee countries beside it. 99.3% of patents are located. The same sections as the author-country gallery, plus inventor-vs-assignee country and the CPC-section mix of each country, and a side-by-side with the paper table.

Patent countries — PatentsView & PATSTAT

PatentsView · PATSTAT 8 figures · patent_country_validation.ipynb

Where patented inventions come from on the two patent indices, under one definition: inventor countries by fractional counting for 8.5 M PatentsView US grants (grant year), 84.9 M PATSTAT applications (filing year) and 52.8 M PATSTAT DOCDB families (priority year). PATSTAT records an inventor address for essentially every US, EP, WO and DE filing but for only about 10% of Chinese and 15% of Japanese first filings, so the family series is also shown filled: an unlocated family takes the country of its national office of first filing. PatentsView is the US lens (US inventors 47.6% of 2010–2019 grants); worldwide, the filled family series gives China 49.7%, Japan 16.5%, Korea 9.0% and the US 9.0% of inventive output. §8 checks that PATSTAT's own US-office grants reproduce the PatentsView shares.

Cross-checks

all families 1 figure · disruption_crosscheck.ipynb

An independent transcription of the CD5 reference algorithm, scored against this pipeline's stored disruption values on the identical cached graphs for papers (OpenAlex) and patents (PatentsView). The reference drops the focal document from the nk set, so the prediction is nkref = nkours + 1; the figure reports how often the counting terms agree exactly and how close CD is under both variants.

Open gaps

read before citing a number

What is missing, what is borrowed, and which results carry a condition. Nothing here invalidates the metrics; all of it changes how a specific number should be quoted.

coverage Papers

Atypicality coverage and computation vintage

The saved figures in sections 3–4 use the corrected merged 1980–2020 run (41,425,041 papers, 14 partitions at export). Section 9 preserves the earlier 2000–2005 run, before the journal-mapping fix; these vintages must not be pooled. Derived tables reports current stored-table counts, while each figure retains its stated analysis window.

updated Papers

Native author-based team size

The team-size figure uses distinct author IDs per paper from paper_author.parquet, built from the OpenAlex authorship snapshot.

known limit Patent→paper

Patent→paper citations carry no usable examiner split

Only 846 of 34,825,379 linkage rows have reftype = exm, so C_examiner_* is effectively always zero. The columns exist; they should not be interpreted.

read with care Papers

Disruption transfers as a ranking, not as a value

Agreement depends on the citation graph and observation window. Consult the current §3c comparison before carrying disruption values or rankings across sources.

read with care Papers

Beauty coefficient is the least portable metric

B depends on the whole citation history and therefore on snapshot coverage. Consult §3c for the current cross-source comparison.

read with care Patents

Citation-network choice changes trajectory shape

Granted-only and extended networks can produce different convexity classifications. Sections 15 and 21–26 show the comparisons; state the citation network with every result.

known limit Paper–patent pairs

The pair list is a choice, not a given

The saved validation figures use the 548,315-pair plus list; the adjusted list contains 42,967 pairs. Pair choice and eligibility filters determine the analysis sample. The Raw data section reports separately verified provenance for the current stored trend outputs. Record both when comparing results or rerunning the pipeline.

coverage Papers — Dimensions

Dimensions atypicality exists for 1990–2000 only

The Dimensions chain has written paper_z_score_1990_2000.parquet and its pair table, not a merged corpus-wide file, so dimension_validation §12 (Z-score CDF and the Uzzi 2×2) is not drawn yet and the Dimensions-vs-OpenAlex comparison in §14 excludes atypicality. Every other Dimensions metric is corpus-wide.

definition Patents — PATSTAT

PATSTAT is stored on two clocks and two units

PATSTAT/output dates both ends of a citation by filing year and keeps every application; PATSTAT/output_grant dates them by grant year and keeps granted applications only, as PatentsView does. About 61% of the filing-clock edges have both ends granted, PCT (WO) applications drop out of the grant clock, and grant lags differ by office (about 2–7 years), so a grant-year window covers different filing vintages across offices. PATSTAT/output_family counts a DOCDB family (one invention, however many offices it was filed at) once, dated by its earliest priority year; an application-level count gives a multi-office invention one node per office and splits its citations among them. State the clock and the unit with every PATSTAT result; §15 compares the clocks and §17–18 the units.

Inventory checked 3 Oct 2026 (UTC) across OpenAlex, Dimensions, PatentView, PATSTAT, pcs, PPP and Case law. Row counts and schemas come from Parquet footers and column examples from the first rows of each table; file sizes and modification times come from filesystem metadata. Figures are saved exports from 11 validation notebooks. Refreshing this page does not recompute metrics or rerun those notebooks. The inventory snapshot and each figure’s analysis coverage are distinct; earlier atypicality exports remain explicitly labelled.

References: Funk & Owen-Smith (2017) disruption · Fang & Evans (2025) Foundation / Extension / Generalization, Generalization and the Rise of System-level Creativity in Science, arXiv:2510.03240 · Wu, Wang & Evans (2019) team size · Uzzi, Mukherjee, Stringer & Jones (2013) atypicality · Kim, Cerigo, Jeong & Youn (2016) technological novelty · Ke, Ferrara, Radicchi & Flammini (2015) sleeping beauties · Marx & Fuegi (2022) Reliance on Science.
